# Suggested status changes (TPTP v10.0.0 -> verified result)

30 problems whose TPTP status appears to be **wrong**, 45 problems whose formulation appears to be buggy (missing axioms or hypotheses), and 435 problems with status Unknown/Open that are now settled. Of these, 7 wrong statuses and 197 settled problems concern problems that include NUM004-0; they are marked in the tables, and their status under a corrected NUM004-0 is in the last section.

> ⚠️ **Warning: the axiom set SET004-0 + SET004-1 + NUM004-0 (Quaife's NBG with ordinals) is inconsistent**, so every one of the 277 TPTP problems that include NUM004-0 is Unsatisfiable. They appear in the tables below with a note; the cause, a fix, and the status of these problems with the fix are in the last section, [Fixing NUM004-0](#fixing-num004-0).

| 🔴 | [**ALG301-1**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/ALG/ALG301-1.p) | ~~Satisfiable~~ | **Open** | no solution file: The single identity is the Equational Theories Project law E5093, which has no nontrivial finite model (Kisielewicz 1997, who mentioned it only as a possible Austin law). Whether it has a nontrivial (necessarily infinite) model is open: the ETP blueprint (chapter 'Order 5 Austin laws') lists E5093 and its dual E41179 in Table 20.2, 'trivial finite models, unknown infinite models', and neither is among the 32 order-5 Austin laws certified in arXiv:2609.05690 (Sept 2026). Its sibling ALG300-1 is Kisielewicz's proven Austin law E28770 (Table 20.1). So the status Satisfiable is unsupported; if ALG301-1 was meant to be another known Austin law, the formula was mis-transcribed. This entry rests on the literature only; it is not mechanically checked. |

## 🟠 Problem formulation appears to be buggy (45 problems)

**Every entry in this table has a verified model**: its solution file contains a Lean model of the problem exactly as written in TPTP v10.0.0 (axioms plus negated conjecture), checked with `Tools/check_lean_model.sh`. So the status of each problem as written is settled: it is not a theorem. The reason, however, is the formulation: a symbol in the conjecture has no (or too few) axioms, or a hypothesis is missing. The suggested change is therefore to **fix the problem**, since as written it does not express the intended theorem (details in the % Status line of each solution file).

| | Problem | TPTP v10.0.0 | Verified model (problem as written) | Cause and suggested fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 🟠 | [**FLD095-2**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/FLD/FLD095-2.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: ℚ): Satisfiable](Solutions/FLD/FLD095-2/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | with u=a/b, v=c/d, w=(a+c)/(b+d), the hypotheses only give b<0<d, and u<=v does not imply u<=w when b+d<0 (a=0, b=-2, c=d=1: u=0, v=1, w=-1). **Fix:** add the missing sign hypothesis on b+d (or on b), as in the intended 'difficult inequality'. |
| 🟠 | [**SET086-6**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET086-6.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET086-6/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the conjecture uses member_of, but no clause of SET004-0 mentions it, so it is an uninterpreted symbol and the conjecture cannot follow; the Lean model interprets member_of(X) as the universal class. **Fix:** add Quaife's axioms for member_of (member_of(X) is the unique member of X when X is a singleton {x} of a set x), as in TPTP's SET090-7. |
| 🟠 | [**SET086-7**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET086-7.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET086-7/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the conjecture uses member_of, but no clause of SET004-0 (nor the lemma clauses of the -7 version) mentions it, so it is an uninterpreted symbol and the conjecture cannot follow; the Lean model interprets member_of(X) as the universal class. **Fix:** add Quaife's axioms for member_of (member_of(X) is the unique member of X when X is a singleton {x} of a set x), as in TPTP's SET090-7. |

| 🟠 | [**SET091-6**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET091-6.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET091-6/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the conjecture uses member_of and member_of1; SET004-0 mentions neither. Adding Quaife's axioms for member_of is NOT enough: the -7 version contains them and is still not a theorem (our Lean model of it). The cause is the uninterpreted member_of1 (the universal class in the model). **Fix:** axioms for member_of1, or the conjecture was meant to use member_of. |
| 🟠 | [**SET091-7**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET091-7.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET091-7/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | member_of is axiomatised here (member_exists1-4, member_of_singleton_is_unique), but the conjecture also uses member_of1, which no clause mentions; the Lean model interprets member_of(X) as the unique member of a singleton of a set, and member_of1(X) as the universal class. **Fix:** axioms for member_of1, or the conjecture was meant to use member_of (with member_of1 := member_of its first clause is the axiom member_exists3). |

| 🟠 | [**SET109-6**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET109-6.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET109-6/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | first and second are constrained only on members of cross products (and in the domain/range clauses), while the conjecture is about first/second of an arbitrary class; in the Lean model they are the universal class on non-pairs. The missing fact, Quaife's convention first(X) = X and second(X) = X when X is not an ordered pair of sets, is essentially the conjecture itself: TPTP's SET113-7..SET116-7 add it as axioms (existence_of_1st_and_2nd_2..5) and are then trivially Unsatisfiable. **Fix:** add that convention (which makes the problem trivial), or a definition of first and second from which it follows. |

With z the universal class, ordered_pair(null_class, universal_class) is the set {{null_class}}, which is not an ordered pair, so x union {it} is not a subclass of the cross product and not a function (Lean model: x = null_class, y = null_class, z = universal_class). **Fix:** add member(y,universal_class) and member(z,universal_class) as hypotheses. |

| 🟠 | [**SET524-6**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET524-6.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET524-6/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the conjecture is that first(x) = x iff second(x) = x (both hold exactly for x not an ordered pair, under Quaife's convention), but first and second are constrained only on members of cross products, so on a non-pair they can differ. In the Lean model x is the universal class (not a pair), first(x) = x and second(x) = null_class (SET524-6), or the mirror image (SET525-6). Same cause as SET526-530. **Fix:** Quaife's convention for first/second on non-pairs (first(x) = second(x) = x). |
| 🟠 | [**SET525-6**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET525-6.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET525-6/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the conjecture is that first(x) = x iff second(x) = x (both hold exactly for x not an ordered pair, under Quaife's convention), but first and second are constrained only on members of cross products, so on a non-pair they can differ. In the Lean model x is the universal class (not a pair), first(x) = x and second(x) = null_class (SET524-6), or the mirror image (SET525-6). Same cause as SET526-530. **Fix:** Quaife's convention for first/second on non-pairs (first(x) = second(x) = x). |
| 🟠 | [**SET526-6**](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/SET/SET526-6.p) | Unknown | [✅ Lean-checked model (infinite: NBG classes over ZFSet): Satisfiable](Solutions/SET/SET526-6/ClaudeOpus---5.5.SAT-Mod.s) | the conjecture says that a class equal to ordered_pair(first(x),second(x)) (or an ordered pair with a proper-class component) has set components, but first and second are constrained only on members of cross products; in the Lean model x = ordered_pair(universal_class,universal_class) is a set that is not an ordered pair of sets, and first and second of it are the universal class. The cause is the same as for SET109-116 and SET119-122. **Fix:** Quaife's convention for first/second on non-pairs. |

| [NUM163-1](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/NUM/NUM163-1.p) | Unknown | Unsatisfiable | [✅ GDV-checked proof (78 steps)](Solutions/NUM/NUM163-1/ClaudeOpus---5.5.UNS-CRf.s) (via the inconsistency of SET004-0 + SET004-1 + NUM004-0, see [Fixing NUM004-0](#fixing-num004-0)). **Note:** this status rests only on the inconsistency. The intended theorem is "the union of two inductive classes is inductive", but the second hypothesis reads K(x) ⊆ y (with K(z) = {∅} ∪ image(successor_relation, z)) where K(y) ⊆ y is surely meant. As written the problem is false in standard NBG: take x = On and y = On ∪ {a} for a set a that is not an ordinal; then K(x) ⊆ x and K(x) ⊆ y, but succ(a) is not in x ∪ y. With K(y) ⊆ y the theorem holds, since K(x ∪ y) = K(x) ∪ K(y). Suggested fix: replace image(successor_relation,x) by image(successor_relation,y) in prove_inductive_closed_under_union_2. |

## Fixing NUM004-0

### The inconsistency

Clause `rest_of4` of NUM004-0 puts (u, restrict(x,u,universal_class)) into rest_of(x) for every u in domain_of(x), even when restrict(x,u,universal_class) is a proper class, while `rest_of1` makes rest_of(x) a subclass of cross_product(universal_class,universal_class), whose pairs have set components (`cartesian_product2`). With x = cross_product(universal_class,universal_class) and u = {null_class}, u x V would be a set, hence so would its range, the universal class; then diagonalise(element_relation) would be a set (Russell's paradox). The 78-step refutation uses only the axioms and is GDV-verified ([NUM289-1](Solutions/NUM/NUM289-1/), the axioms alone). Consequences for the 277 TPTP v10.0.0 problems that include NUM004-0: the 7 with status Satisfiable are wrong (all listed below), the 197 with status Unknown are Unsatisfiable (all listed below; 125 of them also with a direct proof that does not rely on the inconsistency), and the 73 Unsatisfiable ones are trivially so. SUGGESTED FIX: add the literal `~ member(V,universal_class)` to `rest_of4` (the restriction must be a set, as the class existence theorem in the clause's comment requires); requiring function(X) would also do. **With this fix the axioms are consistent**: a Lean model of SET004-0 + SET004-1 + the fixed NUM004-0 is in [Fixed/NUM004-0](Fixed/NUM004-0/) (check with `Fixed/NUM004-0/check.sh NUM289-1`). Of the proofs here, the 125 direct proofs of Unknown problems and the proofs of NUM036-1, NUM097-1, NUM109-1, NUM173-1, NUM175-1 and NUM252-1 do not use `rest_of4`, so they remain valid with the fix. Of the 73 Unsatisfiable problems, 63 have a TSTP derivation that does not use `rest_of4`. One, [NUM219-1](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/NUM/NUM219-1.p) (domain_of(rest_of(u)) = domain_of(u), rating 1.00), is false with the fix (Lean countermodel in the same folder, u = V × V), so it holds only through the inconsistency.

### Status of the 277 problems with the axioms as published and with the fix

With the axioms as published every problem is Unsatisfiable (the axioms alone are contradictory). The columns after the TPTP status give the verifiable status with the fixed `rest_of4` and its evidence: a proof that does not cite `rest_of4` is valid with the fixed axioms unchanged (checked by its leaves, and re-checked with GDV against the fixed axiom file), and a Lean model in [Fixed/NUM004-0](Fixed/NUM004-0/) shows that the conjecture does not follow. With the fix: **195 Unsatisfiable, 22 Satisfiable, 60 Unknown** (Unknown means that the only proofs found so far use `rest_of4`; work on these continues). 🔴 marks a TPTP v10.0.0 status that is wrong for the axioms as published (Satisfiable, although the axioms are contradictory). The status after the fix is new information about a corrected axiom set, not a correction of TPTP v10.0.0.

|  | [NUM163-1](https://seetptp.tptp.org/file/TPTPWorld/TPTP/Problems/NUM/NUM163-1.p) | Unknown | **Unknown** | — (no proof or model with the fix yet). **Note:** this status rests only on the inconsistency. The intended theorem is "the union of two inductive classes is inductive", but the second hypothesis reads K(x) ⊆ y (with K(z) = {∅} ∪ image(successor_relation, z)) where K(y) ⊆ y is surely meant. As written the problem is false in standard NBG: take x = On and y = On ∪ {a} for a set a that is not an ordinal; then K(x) ⊆ x and K(x) ⊆ y, but succ(a) is not in x ∪ y. With K(y) ⊆ y the theorem holds, since K(x ∪ y) = K(x) ∪ K(y). Suggested fix: replace image(successor_relation,x) by image(successor_relation,y) in prove_inductive_closed_under_union_2. |
